## 2026-10-26 — optimal relative-envelope mass factor; second instance of projection reference-kind typo

| Stable finding ID | Severity / status | Pinned evidence, proof and disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **POME-REL-ENVELOPE-MASS-SHARP-001** | low / **verified conditional deduction**, printed Lemma 16.28.69 true | main \`b6933b772f41b45918f7e25ed1afbbb1fd892272\`; \`docs/sections/envelopes-and-volumes-of-singularities/index.html\` blob \`0bdddc04cbc0cefd05591fdffc0796f3405b2a69\` lines **1246–1267**, source \`non-kahler-pluripotential-theory-94A16878\`, tag \`18D2E34A9D42\`. On fixed \(X=\mathbb P^n\), \(\theta=\omega_{\rm FS}\), smooth hyperplane \(H\), \(u=a\log|s_H|_{\rm FS}^2\), \(0<a<1\): \(\theta+dd^cu=(1-a)\theta+a[H]\), \(a\theta+dd^cu=a[H]\). Thus \(P_{a\theta}(u)=u\); non-pluripolar locality and zero mass on \(H\) give \(\langle(\theta+dd^cu)^k\rangle\wedge\theta^{n-k}=(1-a)^k\theta^n\) for all \(1\le k\le n\). Since the Kähler bounded-potential lower mass is \(v_-(\theta)=\int_X\theta^n\), equality holds in the printed top-mass bound. **Proposal:** optional sharpness example following 16.28.69; relevant dependent 16.28.70; no false theorem. [Full proof](reports/2026-10-26-hyperplane-relative-mass-sharpness-and-envelope-system-crossref.md). |
| **POME-IDEAL-PROJECTION-REFERENCE-KIND-001** (new affected location, historical ID retained) | low / **confirmed error, editorial only** | \`docs/sections/envelope-systems-over-hermitian-perturbations/index.html\` blob \`856e240bbc5c86058ff861873f11e87db021e1bd\`, line **65**, proof of Proposition 16.29.3, tag \`160B15BF08FE\`, source \`non-kahler-pluripotential-theory-C6135113\`: “by corollary 18.4.4” points to \`2506B7AD6F19\` = **Proposition** 18.4.4 (\`F9497834\`) in \`docs/sections/multiplier-ideal-envelopes-and-goodness--ideal-projection/index.html\`, blob \`f1979b73c857f4874dd4a5cb10b93bcc047ebbdc\`. Another occurrence at §18.4.5 is already in the ledger: this row tracks a **new location of the same discrepancy**, not a new ID. Replace only “corollary” with “proposition”. The ordinary-envelope argument itself remains valid. [Evidence](reports/2026-10-26-hyperplane-relative-mass-sharpness-and-envelope-system-crossref.md). |

## 2026-10-26 — fixed-root Gauduchon determinant stability and supercritical concentration

| Stable ID | Severity / status | Pinned source, proof and disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **POME-GAUD-FIXED-ROOT-STABILITY-001** | low / **verified conditional deduction**; printed Theorem 12.18.8 remains true | Main b6933b772f41b45918f7e25ed1afbbb1fd892272; docs/sections/a-gauduchon-variational-volume--geometric-bounds-and-determinant-concentration/index.html, blob 6316720e1ce9112dc6b9b2f62db91361cba7e0cc; Theorem 12.18.8 source 6957E040 / tag 3EA495FF9D1A lines 131–196; support identity (12.18.9) source 1F4A2E2C / tag 290803738499 lines 140–145. For fixed smooth positive root r, k_r=C r^(n−1)/||r||_n^(n−1), C=V^(−1/n), q=n/(n−1), every T satisfies ||k_T−k_r||_q ≤ 2^((3−q)/2) sqrt[C Δ_r(T)/((q−1)||r||_n)], where Δ_r= C||r||_n−int r k_T. Complete scalar Taylor-remainder/Hölder proof; optimal square-root exponent realized by smooth closed torus forms. Convergence holds for arbitrary directed near-maximizing nets. Proposed optional corollary, not a false theorem. [Complete proof](reports/2026-10-26-gauduchon-fixed-root-lq-stability-and-concentration.md). |
| **POME-GAUD-CONCENTRATION-LQ-VS-LP-001** | low / **verified conditional deduction**; printed Proposition 12.18.2 remains true | Same pinned main/page; Proposition 12.18.2 source DB141F7B / tag 89AE8B534538 lines 34–96; Equation 12.18.4 source B7A807F6 / tag A89800F03DB4 lines 58–74. Its concentrating fixed-root maximizing family actually has strong L^q density convergence with ||k_S−C||_q ≤ C[2/log(1/epsilon)]^(1/q) and current coefficientwise total-variation convergence O(1/log), while every fixed higher L^p norm diverges. Follows from |a^(1/q)−b^(1/q)|^q≤|a−b| and the exact torus form expansion. Distinct from Theorem 12.18.17's varying exact maximizers; no duplicate finding. Optional explanatory addition, not a correction of false mathematics. [Full proof](reports/2026-10-26-gauduchon-fixed-root-lq-stability-and-concentration.md). |

## 2026-10-25 — distributional Hessian representative and quantitative gradient stability

| Stable ID | Severity / status | Pinned source and disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **POME-DISTRIBUTIONAL-HESSIAN-REP-001** | low / **verified conditional deduction** (not a false theorem) | Current main `b6933b772f41b45918f7e25ed1afbbb1fd892272`, `docs/sections/measures-and-distributions--densities-and-differentiation-of-integrals/index.html`, blob **1b600ca8444edebc8e05e300cb86ae371e9f8535**, Proposition **2.8.5**, source `foundations-of-pluripotential-theory-B959E3ED`, permanent tag **B412D7DC28A3**, exact statement lines **146–151**, proof **154–226**; upstream Lemma **2.8.1** tag **9790D3B21542** lines 10–46, Corollary **2.8.2** tag **D30ED2659FC7** lines 54–75, cube Poincaré Lemma **2.8.4** tag **CAAD90CD1576** lines 124–142. The continuity premise is unnecessary when input is a real distribution: bounded distributional Hessian determines a unique `C¹,¹_loc` representative; convex-set matrix-norm bound gives optimal gradient Lipschitz constant `M`, and printed coordinatewise `dC` is sharp. Affine components are controlled by test-function moments, not by Hessian bounds alone. Propose optional internal corollary only; original statement true. [Full proof](reports/2026-10-25-distributional-hessian-representatives-and-c0-to-c1-stability.md). |
| **POME-DISTRIBUTIONAL-C0C1-MODULUS-001** | low / **verified conditional deduction** (not a false theorem) | Same source/Proposition 2.8.5 tag **B412D7DC28A3** lines **159–181**, and elementary one-dimensional Taylor integrations. Two `C¹,¹` functions on a ball with individual Hessian operator norms ≤`M`, uniform error `δ`, and interior margin `r`, have gradient difference ≤`inf_{0<t<r}(δ/t+Mt)` on that interior, hence ≤`2√(Mδ)` for `δ<Mr²`. The smooth oscillation `δ sin(√(M/δ) x₁)` shows sharpness of the exponent `1/2`. Uniformly converging **directed nets** with a common Hessian bound converge in compact-local `C¹` without sequential compactness. Propose optional stability lemma; original theorem correct. [Full proof](reports/2026-10-25-distributional-hessian-representatives-and-c0-to-c1-stability.md). |

**Precise scope:** first selected integrated proof-level review §§2.8.1–5; additional selected §2.9.2/.4–6/.10–12/.19/.22/.24/.26–28 checked on main blob **b2366d2a9604cc6f39cf45ced697905978dff8ab**, without re-registering prior `POME-FOURIER-PROJ-001`, `POME-FOURIER-INC-001`, or `POME-INCIDENCE-LINES-MASS-001` as new findings. | --- | --- | --- |
| **POME-TUBE-CONVEXLOG-MAJORANT-001** | low / **verified conditional deduction**, printed theorem correct | §9.39.2 tag 7D65E928B1FB lines 27–60 and §9.39.7 tag E5EB0154B387 lines 97–147, docs/sections/normal-spaces-and-tube-continuation--holomorphic-extension-from-a-connected-tube/index.html blob a94e3aa490fee004284dab37f4f6e35094bf2bfa. | **POME-GAUD-STRICT-DOM-GAP-001** | low / **verified conditional deduction**, printed proposition correct | §12.24.1 tag 7CE9EDC18591 lines 10–34, docs/sections/a-gauduchon-variational-volume--orders-of-degeneration-at-the-nef-boundary--positive-current-subtraction-and-divisorial-residuals/index.html blob 8c1eb4eba5af6cc9331b103ed5156251091acce7; §12.2.1–3 tags 0C4AF7E2CEBB, 4AF37F3C8091, 2DB664A6EFDF, docs/sections/a-gauduchon-variational-volume--the-quotient-and-the-smooth-equation/index.html blob 9ec67161d46b3ba20afa1b1c9a0c84f29322763e lines 7–80. Optional quantitative strengthening only; do not claim a positive uniform gap from S>=0. |

**Reviewed main:** `b6933b772f41b45918f7e25ed1afbbb1fd892272`; **starting audit branch head:** `e5cd7f073f0a34a5ceab7fd0e89c561a646d3145`. [Proofs, hypotheses, current main blobs/line anchors, scope](reports/2026-10-25-nonpluripolar-exponential-moduli-and-slow-logarithmic-example.md).

| Stable finding ID | Severity / status | Source, hypotheses, complete argument, proposed addition and affected dependents |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **POME-CAP-DOM-EXP-MODULUS-001** | low / **verified conditional deduction**; no false printed theorem | §5.44.5 `D260E972F57C`, `docs/sections/plurisubharmonic-functions-and-singularities--integration-against-non-pluripolar-measures/index.html`, main blob `15c449ab02d6f8df98edfa07f5286af7ac8e72d1`, lines 48–86; §5.29.2 `5CEF121C5391`, Eq. `3EF8F920CDD3`, `docs/sections/plurisubharmonic-functions-and-singularities--hermitian-capacity/index.html`, blob `77a9098e9bb922425d11c5b04d8927a3997ad44e`, lines 36–77. Finite `mu(E)<=A Cap(E)^gamma` yields uniform `O(lambda^gamma)` for gamma<1, `O(lambda log(1/lambda))` at gamma=1 and `O(lambda)` for gamma>1 by Tonelli/tail integration. **Optional corollary** after §5.44.5; downstream damping needs quantitative capacity domination for this rate. |
| **POME-NONPOLAR-SLOW-EXP-001** | low / **verified conditional deduction**; no false printed lemma | Same §5.44.5 `D260E972F57C`, main lines 48–86 and §5.26.2 `88C7A8DCE36E`, `docs/sections/plurisubharmonic-functions-and-singularities--uniform-exponential-integrability/index.html` blob `d961c0412577bb6abdb8455efc44e2f35760c55c`, lines 18–41. On P¹ let `v_a=a log(|z|^2/(1+|z|^2))`, `0<a<1`. Push forward `dt/[t(log t)^2] dtheta/(2pi)` from `z=e^{-t+i theta}`, t>=e. This is a fixed finite **absolutely continuous** non-pluripolar measure. Exact `lim_{lambda->0} log(1/lambda)*int(1-e^{lambda v_a})dmu=1` by splitting at t=1/lambda and tails t>=M/lambda. Hence *no* positive power rate from bare non-pluripolarity, even fixed v; **optional example**, dependent quantitative claims require new hypotheses. Printed §5.44.5 is true. |

The smooth PDE and quotient theorem remain conditional upstream. |
| **POME-GAUD-CONE-DOUBLE-SCALE-001** | **low / verified conditional deduction**, not a fixed-background counterexample | Proposition 12.26.1 tag `94DF4FEA70D3`, Corollary 12.26.2 tag `87EBA7661A8A`; `docs/sections/a-gauduchon-variational-volume--orders-of-degeneration-at-the-nef-boundary--a-maximum-rank-comparison-criterion/index.html`, blob `ccce6cecc0a14fcf89c2c385e7d8f87ff10df2c8`, lines **10–55**; precursor Lemma 12.25.1 tag `DAA29135206D`, `...integer-orders-in-fixed-projective-cones/index.html`, blob `82790640609d3d58b131873295a827e8d7869d85`, lines **10–58**. For `X=P¹×P^q`, `theta_t=tB1+B2`, `Omega=B1+B2`, the **exact** closed volume is `(q+1)(t+epsilon)(1+epsilon)^q`. Each fixed `t>0` has order 0, `t=0` order 1, but moving `t=epsilon^s` has slope `min(s,1)` for all real `s>0`. Demonstrates parameter nonuniformity, not a false theorem. **Propose optional example** after 12.26.2; affects interpretations of uniform volume-asymptotic and smooth-family variation, not the printed birational fixed-order claim. |

# 2026-10-25 — non-length metric geometry of curve models and complete boundary Kähler potential

**Fresh main:** b6933b772f41b45918f7e25ed1afbbb1fd892272; **UTC checkpoint:** 2026-10-25T00:03:40.568Z. [Full proof report, integrated source tags and physical HTML anchors](reports/2026-10-25-curve-joint-metric-intrinsic-and-boundary-completeness.md).

| Stable finding ID | Severity / status | Proof and exact source; proposed action / dependents |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **POME-CURVE-JOINT-NONMIDPOINT-001** | **low / verified conditional deduction**, no false original theorem | Theorem 13.30.3, source non-kahler-pluripotential-theory **D0548646**, tag **C5551396F93B**, Eq.13.30.1 source **1F6BA73C** tag **8E9F949AEF0D**, Eq.13.30.2 source **F5836858** tag **685F813E23E8**, main **docs/sections/volumes-under-bounded-mass--a-complete-space-over-varying-curve-backgrounds/index.html**, blob **506708195b4fbeb7c1c0ac6ebea922983a764a6b**, lines **15–43**. Take (σ,0), ((1+T)σ,0) with T||σ||_{C^0}>1. Clipped zeroth seminorm has strict triangle inequality through every distinct intermediate background; a midpoint would have to retain an endpoint background and zero polar measure, impossible. The complete joint metric **is not geodesic**, although fixed-background polar fibers are; optional caveat, no source patch. |
| **POME-CURVE-JOINT-NONRECTIFIABLE-001** | **low / verified conditional deduction** | Same Eq.13.30.1–2 and source, lines **15–38**. Every continuous path has D-length ≥Σ_k2^{-k-1}||σ1−σ0||_{C^k} (possibly ∞); proof via sufficiently fine partitions and Minkowski inequality. On a flat elliptic curve, σ0=η and σ1=(1+εsin(2πx))η, 0<ε<1, are positive area-one backgrounds, zero polar measures, finite metric distance, and a continuous straight interpolation, yet every connecting path has infinite length for standard flat derivative norms. Separate completeness from intrinsic-length/rectifiable connectivity in any downstream variational assertion. |
| **POME-PSH-BOUNDARY-COMPLETE-METRIC-001** | **low / verified conditional deduction**, not a correction | Theorem 9.35.1 source demailly-complex-geometry **8B2CF5E4**, tag **AC8A460928B4**, main **docs/sections/boundary-levi-geometry-and-holomorphic-extension--bounded-power-exhaustions-at-a-smooth-pseudoconvex-boundary/index.html**, blob **3c2f1e7af53a9f7c86ca718d47e5054679ff75a7**, lines **11–16,22,41,53,70,74**. On C^\infty bounded pseudoconvex Ω the actual collar power formula gives Φ=−log(−u)=η(−log δ+K|z|²), H_Φ≥(η/2)|∂δ|²/δ²+η|ξ|² and every path to ∂Ω has infinite induced length. Thus dd^cΦ gives explicit complete smooth Kähler metric (up to fixed dd^c normalization). For C² regularity do not assert smooth metric. Existing geometric Oka and Richberg tags **EA195CC9592D**, **52930869627B** remain conditional inputs, not the user's excluded approximation programme. Optional corollary and quantitative note after §9.35. |

For a centrally symmetric compact convex \(E\subset\mathbb R^n\) containing 0 in its real interior and \(0<s\le1\), exact \(\sup_{\mathbb C^n}(V_{sE}-V_E)=\operatorname{arcosh}(1/s)\), attained at **every** \(x\in\partial_{\mathbb R^n}E\). **Reviewed main:** b6933b772f41b45918f7e25ed1afbbb1fd892272. First selected integrated proof review of previously inventory-only §§7.22, 7.26–7.27 and 15.13–15.15, not whole-section certification. | Stable finding | Severity / status | Exact premises, proof, correction proposal, dependents |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **POME-GREEN-HOLO-RETRACT-001** | **low / verified conditional deduction**; not a false theorem | §7.27.2, source klimek-pluripotential-theory-DC0D60AB, permanent **28AAE4E86F18**, docs/sections/symmetric-green-envelopes-on-unbounded-domains/index.html blob **76c965efd42eb17ffa9c34c8b7b0408d56a9e2ce**, lines **28–55**; §7.27.3 source klimek-pluripotential-theory-E575F65F, tag **7F276A63690B**, lines **56–77**. If p:D→Δ and holomorphic i:Δ→D satisfy p∘i=id, **both** g_D and symmetric W_D restrict to the exact disc Green kernel log|(t−s)/(1−conj(s)t)| on i(Δ)×i(Δ), even for unbounded D with collapsed Green slices elsewhere. Proof: K_p is separately PSH, jointly SH and satisfies the inner-ball distance constraint via Schwarz; pulling all Green competitors back along i gives the reverse bound. On the explicit strongly pseudoconvex Hartogs D in §7.27.3, this gives equality for all pairs (0,s),(0,t), without claiming isolated poles or mass normalization. Propose optional retract corollary; affects interpretations of unbounded-domain Green envelopes. |
| **POME-EXP-MA-DENSITY-RATIO-001** | **low / verified conditional deduction**; not a false theorem | §15.13.2, source non-kahler-pluripotential-theory-241F3E4D, permanent **F71978C42841**, docs/sections/equations-with-prescribed-singularities--comparison-above-a-strict-singular-barrier/index.html blob **db560c935a7d651803427b6243957ea1f957b468**, lines **45–99**, and Cor. §15.13.3 source non-kahler-pluripotential-theory-3262185F, tag **24CD508AEB8D**, lines **100–127**. For two e^(λu) density equations in the **same ordinary type** above the fixed strict barrier, if f≤e^εg and g≤e^εf a.e., then sup_X|u_f−u_g|≤ε/λ. One-sided f≤g implies u_f≥u_g. Proof applies strict comparison to u_f against u_g−t for t>ε/λ with c=e^(ε−λt)<1, then switches order. Scaling g=e^εf gives the exact difference ε/λ, hence the constant is sharp. Proposed optional quantitative-order corollary, not a modification of the printed theorem; affects exponential MA density perturbation and uniqueness, not general additive L^p errors. |

| **POME-VOLCOMP-UNIFORM-NETS-001** | **low / verified conditional deduction**, no false printed theorem | §13.22.1 source non-kahler-pluripotential-theory-82BBCEC3, permanent A220D62C8412, main blob 390f280e62ce8ba01de30f6b1ed52b4aa7fb10e4 lines 10–20; §13.22.4 source 6F23D12A / 38359A611D4C, lines 55–90; §13.22.5 source 13BB878E / 412DBAD823B9, lines 97–134 and inequality (13.22.6), permanent DBE56A98BF3E; §13.22.7 source 54605E21 / 62ECCE0A41E6, lines 138–196. On M_delta(theta), the comparison entourages d<epsilon define a **Hausdorff countably based uniformity**, because the printed quantitative quasi-triangle allows V_eta∘V_eta⊂V_epsilon. **Every Cauchy directed net converges**, by extracting a (not assumed cofinal) Cauchy sequence and using the quasi-triangle to control every original tail. Complete argument and hypotheses in linked report. Propose an optional net-completeness corollary after §13.22.7; no claim d is a metric. Dependents: directed model envelopes and nonsequential continuity assertions. |
| **POME-VOLCOMP-LELONG-HOLDER-001** | **low / verified conditional deduction**, no false printed theorem | §13.18.3 source non-kahler-pluripotential-theory-0442EA52, permanent 169DBA6BAF3B, main blob 28323e5b08b0f7e53567f641bd9e4223472bc331 lines 53–73. §13.22.1 definition, blob 390f280e62ce8ba01de30f6b1ed52b4aa7fb10e4 lines 10–20. If u,v have lower singularity volumes ≥delta>0, then for every **fixed** smooth compact modification pi:Z→X, sup_Z|nu(u∘pi)−nu(v∘pi)|≤C_pi min{1,(d_theta(u,v)/delta)^(1/n)}. Proof: put m=max(u,v), use ordered deficit Proposition 13.18.3 separately for u≼m and v≼m; their nonnegative differences from nu(m) each lie in the SAME interval [0,L], so |difference|≤L **without factor 2**. Generic divisorial values obey the same bound. Combined with net completeness gives uniform Lelong convergence of d-Cauchy nets on each fixed modification; constants are NOT uniform across modifications. Propose an optional independent order-free Lelong corollary. Dependents: divisorial/trace singularity data, comparison completions and fixed-model limits. |

# 2026-10-24 — false pointwise Fekete limit in a filtration test curve

**Reviewed main:** b6933b772f41b45918f7e25ed1afbbb1fd892272. New targeted reading of the formerly inventory-only §17.14, including Definition .8 and Example .10; no complete section certified. [Exact proof, source anchors and correction](reports/2026-10-24-filtration-pointwise-fekete-obstruction.md).

| Stable finding | Severity / status | Exact source, counterexample, repair, dependents |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **POME-FILT-FEKETE-LIM-001** | **medium / confirmed error in printed auxiliary pointwise formula; verified local repair** — **not** an error in the maximal-test-curve construction | Integrated docs/sections/test-curves--model-test-curves/index.html at reviewed blob e058100104283d27de55bc64b4ce784f66ace28b, Definition 17.14.8 source sgpt-2BF3A902 / permanent D6CC0BBA6D35, physical lines 144–185; Example 17.14.10 source sgpt-780413CB / permanent 20A8C7BFE0BB, lines 191–272; **Equation 17.14.11 source sgpt-8C49F56B / permanent 79C9CAB675E4, lines 200–209**. On the big line bundle L=O_X(1)⊗p*T over X=P_E(O_E⊕M), with E elliptic, deg M>0 and T nontrivial 2-torsion, the **trivial** allowed filtration gives τ(F)=0. On the quotient section S, every odd-degree section vanishes, whereas an even-degree section and all its powers do not: for every τ<0 the sequence k^(-1)Γ^{F,k}_τ(x) equals -∞ for odd k and remains uniformly bounded below along even k. Thus the displayed ordinary **pointwise limit fails**. Extended-valued superadditivity gives only **limsup_{k→∞} a_k/k = sup_k a_k/k**, proved by the multiples-subsequence argument, with no additional hypotheses. Propose replacing lim with limsup and adjusting the Fekete sentence, preserving all tags and attribution; **not integrated**. The immediately following Γ^F definition already uses the right-hand supremum, so its later concavity/full-mass argument and Remark 17.14.15 are **not** thereby disproved. Prior NA concave-tail defect §22.1.88 is distinct and retained. |

**Exact scope:** actual integrated §17.14.1–.6 and .8–.15, selected proof steps; NA §§22.1.94–.98, .107–.113 checked only for dependency orientation, not certified, and none of their deep analytic inputs reopened. # 2026-10-24 — positive-current extension cones and finite pluripolar restriction

**Reviewed main:** `b6933b772f41b45918f7e25ed1afbbb1fd892272`. First selected proof-level inspection of §§5.44, 5.53, 5.60–5.61 (only specified passages, no entire section certified). [Full English mathematical proof with stable source tags and physical lines](reports/2026-10-24-elmir-extension-cone-and-polar-restriction-boolean-algebra.md).

| Stable finding | Severity / status | Source, justification, correction and dependents |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **POME-ELMIR-EXTENSION-CONE-001** | **low / verified conditional deduction**, not a false theorem or independently certified Skoda–El Mir proof | In §5.60.3, source `demailly-complex-geometry-21C67190`, permanent `BB0A0DD22A5E`, and Cor. §5.60.6, source `demailly-complex-geometry-3BEFC986`, permanent `53F93547F96E`: `docs/sections/positive-current-extension-and-analytic-integration--extension-of-positive-currents-across-complete-pluripolar-sets/index.html`, main blob `6b5be95436780409c8ab09f7f344899ac9047e7a`, physical lines **69–118**. For any positive closed $T$ on $X\setminus E$ with locally finite mass near closed locally complete pluripolar $E$, all positive closed extensions are **exactly** $T^0+R$ with $R\ge0$, $dR=0$, $\operatorname{supp}R\subset E$; $T^0$ uniquely has zero mass on $E$. Nonuniqueness without this condition is witnessed by $0$ and $c\delta_0$ across a puncture in $\mathbb C$ (bidimension zero). For finite $E,F$, coefficient restrictions form additive positive-cone idempotents $P_EP_F=P_{E\cap F}$ and $P_E+P_F=P_{E\cup F}+P_{E\cap F}$. Complete proofs in linked report. Propose **optional** new corollary after §5.60.6, with unchanged body/tags. Dependents: Siu positive-current support decomposition, divisorial trace, b-divisor restriction. |

| Stable finding | Severity / exact status | Source, mathematical proof, correction proposal and dependents |
| --- | --- | --- |

The test-form LF topology on any noncompact second-countable smooth `M` supports a zero-convergent net of nonzero forms with supports eventually outside every compact; every tail is nevertheless unbounded by the printed boundedness criterion. Construction indexed by zero-neighborhoods and exhaustion levels; absorbing neighborhoods permit scalar bumps. Propose optional example clarifying sequences vs nets, not altering the printed sequence assertion. Affects any current/duality proof that assumes common support or boundedness for a convergent **net**. |
| **POME-CURRENT-MOVING-ORDER-001** | **low / verified conditional deduction**; no proof gap | Theorem 2.10.5, same path/blob, source `demailly-complex-geometry-6DD9E811`, permanent `423C948D346B`, lines **95–130**. `T_n=delta_n^(n)` on R converges pointwise on tests to zero; `{0,T_n}` weakly compact. Its supports escape and no uniform global finite order exists, by `epsilon^n chi((x-n)/epsilon)` with n>N. Fixed-K order bounds still hold since only finitely many supports meet K. Proposed sharpness remark, preserving K-dependence; warns against globalizing Banach–Steinhaus bounds. |
| **POME-FINEST-LCS-BOUNDED-SUPPORT-001** | **low / verified conditional deduction**; no false completeness claim | Lemma 2.23.1, `docs/sections/completions-and-locally-convex-quotients/index.html`, main blob `e2c6d25d35020ccefa739f04aa893e173f73c17e`, source `demailly-complex-geometry-C85CEBC0`, permanent `0628F713D150`, lines **11–33**. Yet nonzero scalar basis vectors chosen in increasingly small neighborhoods along the finite-subset directed set form a convergent net with no bounded tail. Optional bornology remark. Affects the proper interpretation of Cauchy nets and completion, not the established complete-space lemma. |

# 2026-10-24 — finite-curve polar retraction degree threshold

**Reviewed main:** b6933b772f41b45918f7e25ed1afbbb1fd892272. New selected integrated §§18.19–18.21 and 18.23–18.24, none certified in entirety. [Source-anchored report with complete measure proof](reports/2026-10-24-finite-curve-polar-retraction-sharp-threshold.md).

| Stable finding ID | Severity / status | Pinned source, justification, proposed correction and dependents |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **POME-CURVE-FINITE-RETRACT-SHARP-001** | **low / verified conditional deduction**, NOT false original theorem or gap | §18.24.2 source non-kahler-pluripotential-theory-6421C90F, permanent tag **C8EE003DDDC9**, main docs/sections/multiplier-ideal-envelopes-and-goodness--a-topology-on-ordinary-singularity-types--models-pulled-back-by-a-finite-curve-map/index.html, blob **dd427a692bda2c364ae83207717a16bcac070cdc**, physical lines **26–63**; §18.24.4 tag **2C40EC6336AD**, lines **89–115**; §18.24.3 tag **F204E0019263**, lines **67–82**. For finite holomorphic \(r:C'\to C\) of degree \(d\ge1\), \(\beta\) smooth real with \(A\ge0\), polar model retraction \(P=d^{-1}r^*r_*\) satisfies \(\|I-P\|_{\rm TV}=2(1-1/d)\) on all finite **signed** measures, with local branch multiplicities respected. For all \(q\), \(d(q,Rq)\le2(1-1/d)\operatorname{dist}(q,J\mathcal M(\beta))\). At \(A>0\), R is nearest for all models exactly for \(d\le2\), and the constant is attained by point-pole analytic ideal models on any unramified \(d\)-sheet fiber when \(d\ge3\); zero area gives singleton. Degree-two nearest models are not unique in general. Proof by the positivity inequality \(r^*r_*\mu\ge\mu\), Jordan decomposition and explicit fiberwise TV minimization. Propose optional post-18.24.2 corollary, keeping original labels and cubic example. Dependents: §18.24.3 finite-factor fibration selection, comparison to §18.20.2 ideal retraction. **Conditional inputs:** curve polar-model classification §13.29.1 and finite-map measure pullback §5.43.16 as already invoked in §18.24.2; no excluded regularization. |

**Reviewed main:** `b6933b772f41b45918f7e25ed1afbbb1fd892272`. **Complete direct mathematical proofs and counterexample:** [English report](reports/2026-10-24-bt-continuous-weights-and-rwn-pole-quantifier.md). | Stable finding | Classification / severity | Precise integrated source, proof, correction and affected dependents |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **POME-BT-CONTINUOUS-WEIGHT-001** | **verified conditional deduction / low**; neither a false theorem nor an unproved printed polynomial case | §7.3.8, source `foundations-of-pluripotential-theory-45C06B5C`, tag `8BD7803A393B`, `docs/sections/bedford-taylor-products-and-local-capacity--decreasing-continuity-and-symmetry/index.html`, blob `96a9e39e85fab5774ebdac0c6d9d006338a4e42c`, lines **234–265**; Theorem 7.3.7, source `foundations-of-pluripotential-theory-8B1F9B78`, tag `D87C13B3250A`, lines **134–148**. For decreasing locally bounded psh sequences `u_{i,j}↓u_i`, `v_{a,j}↓v_a` with locally bounded limits and **fixed** positive closed base current `T`, any **continuous** `H: R^m→R` satisfies `H(v_{1,j},...,v_{m,j}) S_j ⇀ H(v_1,...,v_m) S`, where `S_j` are the Bedford–Taylor mixed Hessian products of the `u_{i,j}`. Proof approximates H uniformly on a fixed compact box of the values by a polynomial and bounds the error by uniform CLN masses (Theorem 7.2.5 tag `2A84C325AB58`, `docs/sections/bedford-taylor-products-and-local-capacity--bounded-potentials-and-recursive-products/index.html`, blob `18bd2395f3b76941b2c677079c09eb6accc5a9cb`, lines **99–118**). Proposed optional corollary after 7.3.8 retaining provenance and original statement; affects bounded nonlinear observables in comparison/contact and variational constructions; not valid as stated for arbitrary unbounded/discontinuous weights or moving base currents. |

# 2026-10-24 — higher-\(C^r\) degenerate densities and weighted separated torus products

**Reviewed main:** `b6933b772f41b45918f7e25ed1afbbb1fd892272`. **Source-anchored mathematical proof:** [dated audit report](reports/2026-10-24-degenerate-density-higher-creg-and-weighted-torus-mass.md). Only selected proofs in §§7.24,7.25,7.33,9.12 and local toric normalization in §21.12.24 inspected. No whole section or transitive PDE theorem certified.

| Stable finding | Severity / exact status | Precise source anchor, hypotheses, justification, proposed action and affected dependents |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **POME-DEGEN-CREG-SCALE-001** | **low / verified conditional deduction**, not a false printed theorem | §7.24.1 permanent tag `D0960BD969B2`, source `klimek-pluripotential-theory-5F0EDAF7`, `docs/sections/degenerate-densities-and-interior-second-derivatives/index.html` blob `fafeab3a344243fe04f24265022bd921f3a4d375`, lines **11–35,37–67,69–100**. For every \(n\ge3\) and integer \(3\le r\le2n-3\), take \(\alpha>2(n-1)/(2(n-1)-r)\) and \(r\alpha<\beta<2(n-1)(\alpha-1)\), then \(f(z',w)=|z'|^\beta\eta(|w|/|z'|^\alpha)\) extended by 0 has \(C^r\) density yet its zero-boundary complex MA solution on the ball fails \(C^{1,1}\) at 0. Derivatives \(D^j f=O(|z'|^{\beta-j\alpha})\), and anisotropic MA/CLN scaling forces \(s(t)-s(0)\ge c\,t^{[2\alpha+2(n-1)+\beta]/(n\alpha)}\) on a contact sequence, exponent \(<2\). Exact \(n=3,r=3,\alpha=8,\beta=26\) gives exponent \(23/12\). **Distinct** from previously logged `POME-KLIM-PLIS-PARAM-001` (feasibility and C² case); this is genuinely stronger density regularity. Conditional on the existing continuous-density Dirichlet, maximality and CLN statements. Optional corollary after 7.24.1, retaining attribution/tag; affects subsequent degenerate MA regularity caveats. The method cannot reach \(r\ge2n-2\), without claiming a general obstruction. |
| **POME-SEPMAX-WEIGHTED-TORUS-001** | **low / verified conditional deduction**, not a false theorem | §7.33.3 tag `F8604F30C7C6`/source `isolated-plurisubharmonic-singularities-DE8582D3`, `docs/sections/separated-maximum-monge-ampere-products/index.html` blob `a859ac5637379266fa191831656f214c51676ca1` lines **39–79**; §21.12.24 tag `725063C1ED05`, `docs/sections/toric-pluripotential-theory-on-ample-line-bundles/index.html` blob `56a1a55dfc8185aee0e6d495a6a93d8a2313d76b` lines **466–529**; Alexandrov normalization §21.5.1 tag `409656CC47D4`. For \(f=\max(0,\lambda_i\log(|z_i|^2/R_i^2))\) on \(\mathbb C^p\), \((dd^cf)^p=(\prod_i\lambda_i)\cdot\mathrm{Haar}_{|z_i|=R_i}\); for another disjoint variable block \(g\), \((dd^c\max(f,g))^{p+q}=(\prod_i\lambda_i\prod_j\mu_j)\cdot(\mathrm{Haar}\otimes\mathrm{Haar})\), **no multinomial factor**. Full subgradient simplex volume \(=(\prod\lambda_i)/p!\) proves the normalization and no mass lies on coordinate axes. This concrete model is distinct from prior abstract multi-block finding `POME-BT-SEPMAX-MULTIBLOCK-001`. Optional example after §7.33.3; affects convex/toric normalization cross-checks. Local BT convergence and Alexandrov mass inputs conditional. |

**Additional selected direct checks without separate new finding IDs:** §7.25.1 tag `B63D9FDC459B`/§7.25.2 `033C98D473B9`, `docs/sections/boundary-zero-green-functions-and-hyperconvexity/index.html` blob `4680003b95a90211015a03b44bd634eb331fb426`, lines **8–33,37–58** (pasting sign and lsc/Green boundary); §9.12.1 tag `362351A63FB6`, `docs/sections/separate-holomorphy/index.html` blob `2eafedb77d4a45f0133a5d92e27ad16b0be44ffd`, lines **8–51** (Baire and coefficient Hartogs). | --- | --- | --- |
| **POME-MODEL-P1-FREEBOUNDARY-EXACT-001** | **low / verified conditional deduction**; **no false theorem or proven proof gap** | §16.21 Definition .1, Proposition .2 and Proposition .3: `docs/sections/envelopes-and-model-potentials--singularity-envelopes/index.html`, blob `8d55c95795ea2a0a3cf5cb81bd4abb4ba867a16a`, physical lines **10–16**, **19–59**, **61–81**; Pome tags `99168E437529`, `037CA226D141`, `5ADA09DD223E`, source `foundations-of-pluripotential-theory` tags `BF5AA585`, `92AD249D`, `38793463`. For \(\omega=dd^c\log(1+|z|^2)\) on \(\mathbb P^1\), \(u_a=a\log(|z|^2/(1+|z|^2))\), \(0<a<1\), exact envelope \(w_a=a\log|z|^2+h(a)-\log(1+|z|^2)\) on \(|z|^2\le a/(1-a)\), zero outside, where \(h=-a\log a-(1-a)\log(1-a)\). Maximality holds for arbitrary competitors by bounded-above subharmonic-removal and the maximum principle. \(\mu_{w_a}=\mathbf1_{|z|^2\ge a/(1-a)}\omega=\mathbf1_{\{w_a=V_\omega\}}\mu_{V_\omega}\), while \(\mu_{u_a}=(1-a)\omega\). Both masses \(1-a\), signed-measure total variation \(2a(1-a)\). **Action:** optional explanatory example to distinguish total-mass preservation from measure equality; no source, proof, registry or tag change. Affected downstream discussion: Proposition 16.21.2(3), Proposition 16.21.3, §§16.20–16.22, toric convention §21.1 (context only). Under `A=integral_0^infty(w(s)/w(0))^(1/n)ds<infty`, the convex composition `F(t)=integral_-infty^t(w(-a)/w(0))^(1/n)da` gives the positive-measure inequality `w(-u) MA_ext(u) <= w(0) MA(F(u))`, hence a **linear** Hermitian capacity bound. For the already constructed smooth `w_j=s_j u_j` with `s_j<=epsilon_j`, normalized `u_j`, and arbitrary prescribed masses, the proof establishes `||w_j||_Lr<=r*C^(1/r)*epsilon_j/(e*alpha)` and `||exp(-p w_j)-1||_Lr<=2*p*r*C^(1/r)*epsilon_j/(e*alpha)` for fixed finite `r>=1`, eventually in `j`. The singular infinite-mass example from Thm 13.14.18 yields an analogous `O(epsilon)` statement for every finite `r`. Strong convergence of potentials/weights does not control MA mass; this does not refute any stated Pome theorem. Propose quantitative corollary/warning only.

# 2026-10-22 — Classical normal-fiber atom jumps; no false printed theorem

**`POME-FIBER-ATOM-JUMP-001` — low printed-theorem risk / medium potential misuse; `verified conditional deduction`.** Reviewed `main` `b8edb6d5fb0f26f5eae0b7b8c8b8d0230c66d9c6`. [Detailed hypotheses, proof, tags, blobs, lines](reports/2026-10-22-normal-fiber-atom-jump-and-finite-germ-dependency-review.md). For every `q>=1,m>=2` on `C^q_z x C_w`, the holomorphic-log tuple `F_m=(z_1,...,z_(q-1),z_q^m,w*z_q)` has pole set `Z={z=0}` and extended-continuous `u=log|F_m|^2`. Classical **normal** top pole mass is `1` for every `w!=0` and `m` for `w=0`, while the global classical current satisfies `1_Z(dd^c u)^q=[Z]`. Exact finite local quotient lengths give the same `1/m` distinction. Thus the “for Lebesgue almost every w” in Lemma **6.48.1** (permanent `D0453276E370`, source `demailly-complex-geometry-AA87475F`, `docs/sections/lelong-densities-generalized-masses-and-multiplicity--normal-fiber-masses-and-integral-logarithmic-coefficients/index.html`, blob `8ab704858d8517df9e20a1bf053364441ef46826`, lines 11–64) is sharp even for smooth `Z` and analytic singularities. The special-fiber jump is arbitrarily large, and **the printed lemma is correct as stated**, not a false theorem or missing proof. Proposed optional explanatory example after Lemma 6.48.1; no direct edits or invented errors in §§6.40,6.43,6.44,6.48,20.2. Internal Remmert link to Thm 9.7.2 `0FC675348681` is already correctly present in §6.43.1 line 22 and §6.44.1 line 16; no false external-dependency claim. Historical/withdrawn audit statuses untouched. §§6.43–6.44,6.46–6.48 received selected proof-level attention only; §6.45 and the excluded dedicated Demailly programme were not reviewed. No project build, CI, formalization or website publication.

# 2026-10-22 — Long Perron geodesic energy versus mixed NPP mass defects (new finding)

**Reviewed main:** `b8edb6d5fb0f26f5eae0b7b8c8b8d0230c66d9c6`. **Stable ID:** `POME-GEO-RELATIVE-SLOPE-MASS-001`; **severity:** low; **status:** `verified conditional deduction` (not an erroneous original theorem and not a fully certified upstream proof chain). [Exact English report](reports/2026-10-22-relative-model-long-geodesic-energy-and-mixed-mass-deficits.md) includes all six inspected integrated page blobs, source labels, permanent tags, physical line anchors, hypotheses, proof, `P^1` check, affected dependents and patch proposal.

For any smooth closed big `(1,1)` background on a compact connected Kähler `X`, positive-mass model `φ` of mass `m`, and `u≤φ`, set `μ_j=⟨θ_u^j∧θ_φ^(n-j)⟩`, `m_j=μ_j(X)`. Relative energy of `u_T=max(u,φ−T)` admits the exact cutoff identity `(n+1)E(u_T)/T = Σ_j[∫(1−(φ−u)/T)_+dμ_j−m]`; consequently the long Perron profile of Prop 17.9.2 satisfies `E(R_s^u)=−s/(n+1)Σ_{j=1}^n(m−m_j)`. The complete nonnegative error is `(n+1)^{-1}Σ_j∫min(1,(φ−u)/T)dμ_j`. Proof uses NPP plurifine locality **only on the strict truncation set**, full mixed mass for the bounded-difference truncations, affine energy in Thm 17.8.3 and true increasing-**net** continuity in Prop 17.4.1. Independent dimension-one `P^1` numerical quadrature checks pass. **Potential addition:** a separately reviewable corollary to Prop 17.9.2. The existing **minimal-reference** special case, Eq. 17.10.94, is retained as distinct historically attributed content, not labelled duplication. Affected dependents include long Perron ray energy interpretation and partial NPP mass theory, **none transitively certified**. The dedicated Demailly approximation programme remains entirely `deferred-by-user`; no source/main/registry/tag edits.

On `Bl_p P²` with `H=π*O(1)` and `E²=-1`, `Vol(H+sE)=1-s²` for `-1<s<=0`, and `=1` for `s>=0`. Thus volume is `C^(1, 1)` but not `C²` at a **big** class; optional sharpness example. Supports exceptional orthogonality and zero restricted volume in 20.8.16 `7ED04B77FCC6`. Does **not** contradict the printed C1,1 theorem. |

Frostman growth `rho(B_r)≤Ar^(m-beta)` gives `||h_t rho||∞≤C(A+mass)t^(-beta/2)` for `0≤beta≤m`; `t∫(h_t rho)F→0` for all nonnegative `F∈L1` when `beta≤2`. On flat tori of `m≥3`, explicit radial densities `rho∼|x|^(-beta)` and `F∼|x|^(-alpha)`, `m+2-beta<alpha<m`, make it diverge for every `2<beta<m`; a Dirac pole handles `beta=m`. No reopening of excluded regularization. Optional corollary after 3.10.5; compact heat existence remains upstream conditional. |

| --- | --- | --- |
| `POME-OKO-INCREASING-TC-ENDPOINT-001` | **High; confirmed error — false proposition as written** | `docs/sections/the-theory-of-okounkov-bodies--okounkov-test-curves/index.html`, blob `4d851dfc4eb1e6bb71937db45624021947e31473`: Definition 19.6.1 tag `ACD722F45231`, endpoint clause `114CD3E176E9` (HTML lines 17–21), and Proposition 19.6.16 tag `D70EFA3100C9` (lines 278–326; unproved limiting-TC claim line 289). With `Δ^i=[0,1]`, `Δ^i_max=i`, `Δ^i_τ=[0,1]` for τ<i, every indexed curve satisfies all hypotheses, but the limit endpoint `+∞` is forbidden by the TC definition; Legendre functions `G_i≡i` also escape to `+∞`. **Add explicit finite upper bound `sup_i Δ^i_max<∞`**; preserve original tags/attribution. Related §19.6.23 tag `22B2EF8281A7` (HTML 411–445) has an ambiguous inherited existence/endpoint condition, **not independently declared false**. |
| `POME-OKO-INCREASING-TC-REPAIR-001` | **Medium; verified repair (proposed, not integrated)** | With `L=sup_i Δ^i_max∈ℝ`, fixed-level monotone compact convex slices have Hausdorff limits; Minkowski concavity and `τ→−∞` normalization survive the limit. The proof of §19.6.16's pointwise Legendre convergence is then valid on `Int Δ`. For **increasing sequences** satisfying the corrected hypothesis, §19.6.23's Duistermaat–Heckman convergence is actually **narrow** (`C_b(ℝ)`), by fixed-body bounded dominated convergence. Without the hypothesis, the counterexample gives DH measures `δ_i`, vaguely escaping to 0 and having no narrow weak limit. No net-DCT claim and no full theorem certification. |

A constant real `q`-form is integral exactly when its coefficients in the ordered `dx_I` basis are integers, in all degrees; the unique constant representative of any closed form has the same criterion. Proof iterates the flat *ordinary* Künneth sequence for `S^1`, uses the **multiplicative Theta** rather than sign-shifted Psi, and projects onto the integral exterior monomial basis. The divisibility of a nonzero PD class is exactly the gcd of lattice coordinates, so primitivity is preserved; proof by unimodular wedge pairing. Add a torus-only corollary after §10.74.3, not a torsion-free claim on arbitrary manifolds. |

# New mathematical findings — 21 October 2026; principal analytic cycles (main `b8edb6d5fb0f26f5eae0b7b8c8b8d0230c66d9c6`)

The complete hypotheses, proofs, exact original source IDs, permanent tags and HTML line anchors are in [reports/2026-10-21-principal-cycle-pushforward-and-local-zero-conservation.md](reports/2026-10-21-principal-cycle-pushforward-and-local-zero-conservation.md). **These are new conditional deductions, not two reported errors in printed theorems.**

| Stable ID | Severity / status | Precise mathematical finding and proposed change |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `POME-CYCLE-PRINCIPAL-PROJECTION-001` | Low; **verified conditional deduction** | `docs/sections/divisors-and-logarithmic-limits-on-analytic-cycles/index.html`, blob `f693f40023ea7e213b7329e02bf4c70308fa0556`, Lemma 20.5.3 `323D5DA3E240` lines 27–63 and Thm 20.5.5 `F1C928146DBC` lines 64–132. If $F:M\to X$ is holomorphic and proper on irreducible hypersurface $D$ of dim $r\ge1$, $B=F(D)$ has dim $r$, $F_*[D]=d[B]$, and $f|_B$ is nonzero, then $F_*\operatorname{Div}_D(f\circ F)=d\operatorname{Div}_B(f)$. Proof uses weighted-log projection identity (with truncations), properness, and commutation of $dd^c$ and pushforward; independent of regularization. Place as corollary to 20.5.5. Dependents: §20.5.10 and §20.7.3 multiplicity compatibility. |
| `POME-CYCLE-LOCAL-ZERO-CONSERVATION-001` | Low; **verified conditional deduction** | Same 20.5.5 tag `F1C928146DBC`, lines 64–132, plus 20.5.10 `DDB973C0C1DD` lines 133–149. For a fixed reduced analytic curve, a locally uniformly varying holomorphic function with an isolated limiting zero at $a$ has exactly the same total integer multiplicity in an isolating neighborhood for all sufficiently large indices. Use collar zero-exclusion, positive integer zero cycles and weak convergence from 20.5.5. Example $A=(x=0)$, $f_\varepsilon=x-y^k-\varepsilon$ has $k$ simple moving intersections merging to one of multiplicity $k$. Suggest curve-only corollary after 20.5.5; no generalization to varying $A_j$ or nonisolated intersections. |

# 21 October 2026 — contracting Hopf quotients (`main` `b8edb6d5fb0f26f5eae0b7b8c8b8d0230c66d9c6`)

**New section in this pass:** §23.3, reviewed exact Theorem 23.3.3 statement and complete printed proof. The original theorem was **not** found false. The two findings below are new **conditional deductions**, without changes to the canonical mathematical body or deduplication.

| Stable finding ID | Severity / status | Source anchors, verified extension, dependents and action |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `POME-HOPF-TRIANGULAR-LINEARIZATION-001` | Low; **verified conditional deduction** (elementary exact polynomial conjugacy), not an erroneous original theorem; proposed remark | `docs/sections/contracting-hopf-quotients/index.html`, blob `74e8166076c658ce0ef9faa0e7552d13d440b192`, §23.3 Theorem 23.3.3, tag `D81E3145A1B4`, source `demailly-complex-geometry-371996B7`, statement lines 9–23 and proof lines 24–64. For `0<|λ|<1`, `p≥2`, `F(z,w)=(λz,λw+z^p)`, polynomial automorphism `H(z,w)=(z,w+z^p/(λ−λ^p))` satisfies `H∘F=(λI)∘H`; corresponding quotient biholomorphic to the scalar Hopf surface. For `p=1` the derivative is nondiagnonalizable Jordan, so no holomorphic *germ conjugacy* to a diagonal map; does **not** assert quotient non-biholomorphism. Add independent remark, preserve provenance/tags; impacts §23.3 interpretation. [Proof](reports/2026-10-21-hopf-triangular-linearization-and-smooth-topology.md). |
| `POME-HOPF-MAPPING-TORUS-001` | Low; **verified conditional deduction** (explicit smooth global trivialization; not a false theorem); proposed corollary | Same full theorem/source/path/tags, statement/proof lines 9–64. For all listed diagonal/triangular contractions, `X_F≅_diff S³×S¹`. Scalar/diagonal and Jordan cases arise as `A=e^B` for stable complex matrices B; set `Q=∫_0^∞e^{tB*}e^{tB}dt`, then `B*Q+QB=-I`. Every `e^{tB}`-orbit crosses the ellipsoid `z*Qz=1` uniquely, furnishing `E×R≅C²\0` and quotient `E×S¹`. Thus `(b0,b1,b2,b3,b4)=(1,1,0,1,1)`, `χ=0`, and no Kähler metric also follows from `b2=0`/Stokes. Relevant comparison §23.2.24 tag `32084430C8AD`, but separate dimension 3 case; proposed corollary after §23.3.3. [Proof](reports/2026-10-21-hopf-triangular-linearization-and-smooth-topology.md). |

Any prescribed local cycle representing a compatible family of cohomology classes can be *approximated* by local coordinates of global compatible cycles representing the same family. Analogous dense approximation holds for any local lift of a compatible element of inverse-limit quotient spaces in strict short exact systems with dense kernel transitions. Proof: global existence + density of inverse-limit cochain projections; difference of cycle representatives is an actual boundary, corrected by global coboundary. **Not equality:** weighted Hilbert scale `H_n` shows inverse-limit projections may be proper dense. For the nonreduced analytic point `A=O(D)/(z^2)` and `ε=[z]`, the *quotient* radius-`t` supremum seminorm is exactly `p_t(ε)=t>0`, by Cauchy estimate on any lift and equality for `z`; pointwise evaluation on reduced support would incorrectly give zero. The integrated proof already uses quotient seminorms, so it is correct on this point. It calls them “multiplicative” although it demonstrates `p_t(ab)≤p_t(a)p_t(b)`; strict equality is impossible for `ε²=0`, but project terminology may use “multiplicative” to mean submultiplicative. Clarify definition `inf` over lifts and term “submultiplicative” if intended. No premature error assertion. Potential dependents §§9.46.7, 9.47.2 still conditional. [Dated proof](reports/2026-10-21-inverse-limit-relative-lifts-and-nonreduced-norms.md). |

Reviewed `main` `a80e9a0e2104a4006b6cfffb8184ee0aca07f3fe` and existing audit branch `0e341985391914cd02305255af14df2d6c5eec1f`. [Detailed report](reports/2026-10-20-okounkov-rankzero-and-lck-volume-degree.md); new findings verified **conditionally on exactly identified internal statements**, no source theorem declared false. Older items retain status.

| Stable ID | Severity / status | Precise source anchors, hypotheses, proof and correction |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **POME-OKO-RANKZERO-ALL-DEGREES-001** | Low; **verified conditional deduction** | §19.9 Prop. 19.9.19 tag `C8B2CB1B6819` at `docs/sections/flags-convex-bodies-and-section-growth/index.html:394–429` (blob `ed745669d969ec5c71d06b0c33f808c5d27ba59e`), Cor. 19.9.20 tag `14480FFA5B57` at `:434–450`. If a smooth curvature form has positive semipositive rank zero on its semipositive locus, any two independent sections in one degree would give a nonconstant section-ratio map with differential rank 1, contradicting the proposition; **`h0(X,L^p)≤1` for all p≥1**, zero if the semipositive locus is empty. Suggest optional all-degree corollary; max-rank proposition itself still relies on its recorded geometric inputs and is not fully transitively certified. |
| **POME-LCK-SEMIPOS-RANK-DEGREE-001** | Low; **verified conditional deduction** | §23.2 Prop. 23.2.7 `11EBB7AD1165`, eq. 23.2.8 `35851308DD2B`, Prop. 23.2.14 `E5F423FF7707`, Thm. 23.2.15 `B8583A0E17A6`, `docs/sections/locally-conformally-kahler-geometry/index.html:98–125,190–230` (blob `7a4928f4c2fb0675eed6db28aa3039fcb54f7e48`). For compact strictly LCK n≥2 with **semipositive** `κ_λ`, `1≤r=max rank κ_λ≤n−1`; every wedge coefficient `∫κ_λ^j ω^(n−j)>0` for j≤r, higher vanish, hence `deg V(t)=r`, `t^(-r)V(t)->binom(n,r)∫κ_λ^r ω^(n−r)>0`, and `t V'(t)/V(t)->r`. Add conditional corollary to Thm. 23.2.15; no unconditional curvature semipositivity claimed. |

The new finding does not duplicate previous b-divisor coefficient or pullback issues.

| Stable ID | Severity / status | Integrated anchor, evidence, affected material / remedy |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **`POME-CSPACE-PRINC-RESID-NONEXC-001`** | **Low–medium; confirmed error** in proof-only assertion, **verified repair proposed, not integrated** | §9.7 Theorem 9.7.5, permanent tag `689628C7AF7F` (source label `1524D010`), `docs/sections/complex-spaces-and-modifications--proper-images-and-relative-principalization/index.html:120` (blob `c6f0b3e1871d0db3307802af387797da0f71a84d`). Step 3 says `H'` is supported on exceptional divisors of `g`; false for `M=P¹×P¹`, `W=P¹×{0}`, `a=I_D` with `D=P¹×{∞}`: first stage empty, Cartier blowup identity, `H'=D` maps to hypersurface. §9.6 Prop. 9.6.3 tag `865FE5EC97E6` (line 50), principalization Thm. 9.6.7 tag `24330D25741D` and eq. 9.6.8 `012823948235` (lines 91–100) explicitly permit Cartier centers. Replace by `Supp H'=g^{-1}(V(a_t))`, disjoint from `W'`; do not call `H'` exceptional. §9.6 line 100's “exceptional factor” has same terminological defect. **H remains exceptional; equations 9.7.6–9.7.8 and Theorem 9.7.5 remain valid.** Affected consumers: any argument inferring residual exceptionality from this sentence; no such specific downstream false theorem verified this pass. |

**Derived conditional criterion:** if `g:N'→N` is proper bimeromorphic between smooth manifolds and `b O_{N'}=O(-B)`, then `ord_{D'} B=ord_D(b)` for each divisorial component `D` of `V(b)` (strict transform `D'` via §9.8.3 tag `2312A839D109`), and `B` is `g`-exceptional iff `V(b)` has no divisorial component. | Stable ID | Severity / status | Statement, hypotheses, precise location, proposal and dependents |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **POME-FINS-HORIZONTAL-NEG-001** | Low; **verified conditional deduction** | Theorem 11.9.4 permanent tag `C222D1BB3C65`, `docs/sections/gauduchon-metrics-and-linear-mass--convex-norms-and-gauduchon-degrees/index.html:123–185`. Its split-degree/adjoint proof gives `I_A(G) <= -lambda_*/4 int_X N_G mu` uniformly over smooth squared complex norms for `C^4`-nearby integrable Dolbeault operators. For every fixed positive base form `chi`, let `K=max_{(x,v) in Sigma} sum_s rho_s(v) (chi wedge Theta_s)/mu > 0` and `c=lambda_*/(8K)`. **For each norm** there exist `x,v,xi` (with `sum_s rho_s(v)>0`) such that its Chern-horizontal Levi form satisfies `H^*(dd^c G)(xi,bar xi) < -c N_G(x) chi(xi,bar xi)`. Proof: otherwise positivity of `Theta_s` would force `I_A(G)>=-lambda_*/8 int_X N_G mu`, a contradiction. Stable under rescaling norms. Conditional on the printed uniform integral estimate, **not** a new false theorem, full upstream certification or source repair. ## New mathematical findings — 20 October 2026, flat-torus Gromov–Hausdorff limits

Pinned `main`: `3cfabb25a59bd0478afe43419e20ae5fa320d159`. The source is the **integrated** reading edition; no change is made to previously confirmed errors, gaps or deferred-by-user work. [Full new mathematical report](reports/2026-10-20-flat-torus-gh-limit-and-rational-diameter.md).

| Stable ID | Severity; status | Exact source, hypotheses, conclusion and next action |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **POME-TOR-FLAT-GH-001** | Low; **verified conditional deduction**, not a false theorem | `docs/sections/bott-chern-classes-and-singular-loci--rigid-boundary-classes-on-a-torus/index.html:10–61`, blob `10147d724411ba92736e619e330f82f4a6065d40`; Theorem 10.26.1 tag `9A07C571BD3F` (source label `DA9C52EB`), and Example 10.26.3 tag `87A3AA3B13FD` at lines 91–115. For a complex torus `X=V/Λ`, constant semipositive `θ`, constant Kähler `κ`, and `H=closure(image ker θ)`, the flat distances of `θ+tκ` converge **uniformly** on `X²` to the pullback of the canonical flat metric on the real quotient torus `X/H`. Thus `(X,d_t)` Gromov–Hausdorff converges to `X/H`, and the diameters converge to the quotient diameter. Proof uses explicit lattice-distance infima and Dini's theorem, avoiding unjustified limit/inf interchange. Do not generalize to arbitrary nonmonotone positive families. Add optional strengthening after the theorem; keep existing source attribution and tags. |
| **POME-TOR-RATIONAL-DIAM-001** | Low; **verified conditional deduction**, not a false theorem | Same path `:66–86`; Example 10.26.2 tag `5B00F62ACA6B` (source label `D92FFC57`). For `θ_a=(i/2)(dz₂-a dz₁)∧(d\bar z₂-a d\bar z₁)` on the standard square two-torus, if `a=p/q` reduced (`q≥1`) and `κ` is any fixed constant Kähler form, then `lim_(t↓0) diam(X,θ_a+tκ)=1/(sqrt(2)q)`. Bézout gives the quotient period lattice `(1/q)(Z+iZ)`; for irrational `a`, the limit remains zero. Optional quantitative addendum to the existing example; no claim about Example 10.26.3's nonmonotone family. |

**Coverage:** targeted §§10.26–10.27; contextual §§3.6–3.7, 22.1; no chapter certified, no Demailly/regularization-related proof work, no full tests or build.

| Stable ID | Severity / status | Exact integrated source and conclusion |

| **POME-RULED-ALG-INDECOMP-001** | Low; **verified conditional deduction**, not a false theorem or an independently certified extension construction | `docs/sections/a-gauduchon-variational-volume--orders-of-degeneration-at-the-nef-boundary--symmetric-tensors-on-split-elliptic-ruled-surfaces/index.html:28–195`, Lemmas 12.31.1 `2604E6F7AC76`, 12.31.2 `711AF6E8C0D8` and Theorems 12.31.3 `654B70C2AFD7`, 12.31.4 `3B7C9C492833`; `docs/sections/a-gauduchon-variational-volume--orders-of-degeneration-at-the-nef-boundary--section-algebras-of-cotangent-extensions/index.html:73–134`, Theorem 12.32.2 `77C35E15A510`, Cor. 12.32.3 `37D7893F0652`; `docs/sections/a-gauduchon-variational-volume--orders-of-degeneration-at-the-nef-boundary--cotangent-extensions-with-zero-ruling-component/index.html:55–71`, Prop. 12.33.2 `A78CB65BF75E`. For `d=deg A>0`, ruling index `k` and tensor degree `r`, the exact bivariate Hilbert series is `H_T(t,z)=1/(1-t)+dzt/[(1-t)^2(1-zt)^2]` and `H_V=H_T/(1-t)` for **both** zero/nonzero ruling components. The degree-`r`, index-one part of `R_+/(R_+)²` is canonically `H⁰(E,A)` of dimension `d`, for every `r>=1`; consequently at least `d` new **bihomogeneous weight-one** generators are necessary at each degree; degree-homogeneous but mixed-weight generators need only independent weight-one projections. This only sharpens the already-known nonfinite-generation theorem; it does not identify all other weight-index indecomposables, identify distinct torsors geometrically, or resolve upstream GAGA/elliptic cohomology. Proposed optional corollary/remark, preserving permanent tags and different hypotheses. Full proof: [dated report](reports/2026-10-20-ruled-surface-weighted-hilbert-series-and-indecomposables.md). |

For fixed smooth positive 2-periodic `F` and `m=n-1`, radial period `L(k)=-int F/C_k^(m+1)` is a smooth strictly increasing diffeomorphism from `(-∞,0)` to `(-∞,0)` with `L'(k)=int F (partial_k C_k^(m+1))/C_k^(2m+2)>0`. For `c=-k>0`, `A=int F` and `f0=min F, f1=max F`: `A c^(m+1)/f1^(m+1) <= abs(L) <= A c^(m+1)/f0^(m+1)`; sharp when F constant. Proof differentiates a uniformly stable periodic scalar ODE. Suggest optional corollary; Thm remains correct, no nonradial uniqueness claimed. Affects 23.2.43–45. |
| **POME-NORM-MONOM-CONDUCTOR-001** | Low; **verified conditional deduction** | `docs/sections/complex-spaces-and-modifications--reduced-spaces-and-normalization/index.html:56–89,116–160,197–203`, blob `d4d1c1d847f77490cfe51f92d94bd29d48989051`, Prop 9.4.3 `36422A732754`, Lemma 9.4.5 `58E87F89D557`, Thm 9.4.6 `12A47DB401E2`, Ex 9.4.10 `60A14C7A1845`; context curve parametrization §9.5.1 tag `A3A9384DC317`. For coprime `2<=p<q`, normalization of `w^p=z^q` is `C{t^p,t^q} ⊂ C{t}`, conductor exactly `t^((p-1)(q-1)) C{t}`, delta `((p-1)(q-1))/2`. Semigroup Apéry classes yield a complete proof. Original example cusp is correct; optional generalized conductor example; affects examples of normalization/branch invariants in §§9.4–9.5. |

**Reviewed main SHA:** `3cfabb25a59bd0478afe43419e20ae5fa320d159`  
**Audit branch at read:** `0e341985391914cd02305255af14df2d6c5eec1f` (preexisting branch preserved).

| Stable finding ID | Severity and status | Mathematical result, exact anchor, dependencies and proposed action |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **`POME-NPP-FINITE-DEGREE-001`** | Low printed-source risk; medium warning; **verified conditional deduction**, plus **verified obstruction to an unasserted generalization** | `docs/sections/non-pluripolar-products--fiber-integration-and-modifications/index.html:36–56` blob `8aeca31e5b85246acb633b01abff1cddbc6f3811`, Prop 13.6.3 tag `0811FFDF15B7`. For a finite surjective holomorphic map of generic degree `d` between connected compact Kähler n-folds, `f_*⟨∧ f^*T_i⟩=d⟨∧T_i⟩`. Proof: on the unramified locus there are `d` inverse sheets; neither non-pluripolar product charges analytic critical loci. The **other** printed identity, with pushforwards of arbitrary factors, relies on birationality; the smooth map `([s:t],[u:v])↦([s^d:t^d],[u:v])` on P¹×P¹ gives a `d` versus `d²` counterexample to extending it to finite maps. Original printed theorem correctly assumes modification. Proposed optional corollary and sharp warning; affects §§5.6, 13.2, 13.5–13.6, 20.1–20.4. |
| **`POME-REL-CAP-FINITE-DEG-001`** | Low; **verified conditional deduction** | `docs/sections/relative-extremals-on-bounded-hyperconvex-domains/index.html:97–106,111–172` blob `e59a93136ce31022f649c96c205b2d2160d94478`, Prop 7.16.4 tag `CF1B00103D5B`, Thm 7.16.5 tag `08DA18E923C1`. If `F:Ω→Ω'` is finite proper surjective of degree `d`, with Ω and Ω' bounded connected hyperconvex and `E⊂⊂Ω'`, `Cap^*_Ω(F^{-1}E)=d Cap^*_{Ω'}(E)` (inner capacity too for compact E). Proof: `H_{F^{-1}E}=H_E∘F` plus BT top-degree pushforward off the branch locus and noncharging of the branch set. `z↦z^d` and closed disc radius r give exact `d/[2log(1/r)]`. Proposed optional corollary; upstream §7.16 and BT not certified in entirety. |

# New stable findings — 19 October 2026: P¹ ray normalization and exceptional pullback tails

Reviewed `main` `3cfabb25a59bd0478afe43419e20ae5fa320d159`. Full justifications and proof line anchors: [reports/2026-10-19-projective-ray-speed-and-exceptional-pullback.md](reports/2026-10-19-projective-ray-speed-and-exceptional-pullback.md). Prior ledger and all statuses retained verbatim after this insertion. These are **not** two erroneous original theorems.

| Stable finding ID | Severity; status | Exact integrated location, hypotheses, verification, proposed correction and dependencies |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **`POME-GEOD-CP1-SPEED-001`** | Low; **verified conditional deduction** | `docs/sections/test-curves-and-legendre-duality/index.html:184–246`, blob `9b7ad285b6ce5139a4f8dc4f25b901ae03aedbea`, Prop 17.17.11 tag `377F0AD20B28`, formula 17.17.12 tag `9BA75154C113`. For normalized P¹ FS ray `U_t=log((1+e^t|z|²)/(1+|z|²))`, `q_t=∂ₜU_t` pushes its own MA measure to Lebesgue on [0,1], hence `E(U_t)=t/2`, `d₁(U_s,U_t)=|s−t|/2` and every moment is `1/(p+1)`. Proof: radial Jacobian `dμ=dq`, first-variation formula §17.3.4 `9086ABF36774`, ordered metric §17.10.41 `08EFAEA550B1`. **Proposed:** optional corollary/benchmark; original theorem correct; dependents §17.3, §17.10, §22.1 DH normalization. |
| **`POME-BDIV-COEFF-PULLBACK-001`** | Low source-severity, high dependency caution; **verified conditional deduction** | `docs/sections/divisorial-currents-on-compact-modifications/index.html:483–539`, blob `2e1a409ba3fec78ae0c4fc7b9b9a83df99f49289`, Thm 20.4.26 tag `400A4F39FD56`, Prop 20.4.22 `D7373D3639FA`, Cor 20.4.24 `AC7947F570DB`. Let `g:BlₚP²→P²` blow up p and `L_m` distinct lines through p. Coefficients of `[L_m]` at each fixed divisor tend to zero; FS masses stay 1, and `g*[L_m]=[L̃_m]+[E]` retains exceptional coefficient 1. Therefore **coefficientwise convergence alone** cannot justify pullback or exceptional limits. The printed theorem is **not contradicted**: it uses actual trace-mass convergence/positive tails and the continuous pullback bound. **Proposed:** explanatory note never to drop this hypothesis; depends on §§20.4.2, 20.4.22, 20.4.24, 20.4.26, 20.4.30. |

Optional explanation supporting normalization by `m_ε`; dependent zero-mass/singularity-envelope estimates. Does not certify §17.5 existence or whole §17.6. |
| `POME-NA-GRADED-CANON-001` | Low; **verified conditional deduction** | §22.1 Lemma 22.1.94 `76252E7717AC`, equation `6126C03E6587`, lines 1403–1414, `docs/sections/non-archimedean-pluripotential-theory/index.html`, blob `eae5173c58fcbbb912f5ef7a51e2536e13b59dd3`. # New finding — 21 October 2026: sharp atomic-pole rooftops

| Stable ID | Severity and status | Exact audited sources, complete claim and downstream context |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **`POME-ROOF-ATOMIC-THRESHOLD-001`** | Low; **verified conditional deduction**, **not a false theorem**, **not a repaired proof** | §16.23 Prop. 16.23.1 tag `3499B9FD713C` and Thm. 16.23.5 tag `641CB44C78E9`, `docs/sections/envelopes-and-model-potentials--rooftops-and-mass-inequalities/index.html`, blob `5c0ac72ae39f166811e570565032c558145f7262`, HTML lines 11–43 and 131–177. Projective-line FS potentials `u_a=a log(|z|²/(1+|z|²))`, `v_b=−b log(1+|z|²)` with `0<a,b≤1`: `P_ω(u_a,v_b)` exists iff `a+b≤1`; when it exists its non-pluripolar mass is `1-a-b` and equality is attained in the printed roof mass inequality. At `a+b=1`, the rooftop is exactly `a L₀+b L∞` and has zero mass. At `a+b>1`, positive current cohomological mass obstructs a common minorant. For q distinct points same existence/mass formula with `Σa_i` holds for this *special family*, not as an unproved universal q-ary theorem. Dependencies: project convention `dd^c`, Poincaré–Lelong, dim-one non-polar restriction and monotonicity; compared §16.22.11 tag `4A1DC8C4E020` and §17.6.5 tag `C6C3A19AAB0D`, distinct valid positive-/zero-mass scopes; §17.7.8 tag `2FECBE87C487` downstream rooftop consumer. **Proposed correction:** none; optional sharpness example after Thm. 16.23.5, preserve tags and attribution.
